\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{u}_{k}\rightarrow\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\text{ strongly in }C\left([0,T];L^{2}_{\xi}(1,M)\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{q}_{k}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}\tilde{q}\text{ weakly* in }L^{\infty}\left([0,T];H^{1}_{\xi}(1,M)\right)

\displaystyle,

(2.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{q}_{k}\rightarrow\partial_{t}\tilde{q}\text{ strongly in }C\left([0,T];L^{2}_{\xi}(1,M)\right).

Moreover, using a diagonal argument and further passing to a subsequence, we can assume that
(
2.47
-
2.50
) hold for each
M>1
.
Meanwhile, applying Azela-Ascoli theorem on
\{f(R_{k})\}
and
\{\partial_{t}f(R_{k})\}
yields the existence of a subsequence of
\{f(R_{k})\}
, still denoted by
\{f(R_{k})\}
, and a limit point
\tilde{f}
such that

f(R_{k})\rightarrow\tilde{f}\text{ in $C^{1}[0,T]$}.

Then we construct the solution
(u,\,q,\,R)
as follows:

R(t):=f^{-1}\tilde{f}(t),\quad r(x,t):=r_{in}(x)+\int_{0}^{t}u(x,s)ds,

u(x,t):=\tilde{u}((1+3x)^{\frac{1}{2}},t),\quad q(x,t):=\tilde{q}((1+3x)^{\frac{1}{3}},t).

To verify
(u,\,q,\,R)
is a solution to (
1.9
-
1.14
), we denote
\tilde{r}_{k}(\xi,t):=r_{k}\left(\frac{\xi^{3}-1}{3},t\right)
, and
\tilde{r}(\xi,t):=r\left(\frac{\xi^{3}-1}{3},t\right)
.
From the inequalities

|\tilde{r}_{k}-\tilde{r}|(\xi,t)\leq\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{u}_{k}-\tilde{u}|(\xi,s)ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{u}_{k}(s)-\tilde{u}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}ds,

\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{r}_{k}-\partial_{\xi}\tilde{r}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{u}_{k}(s)-\partial_{\xi}\tilde{u}(s)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}ds,

it follows that
\tilde{r}_{k}
converges point-wise to
\tilde{r}
, and
\partial_{\xi}\tilde{r}_{k}-\partial_{\xi}\tilde{r}
converges to
0
in
L^{2}_{\xi}
due to (
2.47
) and the embedding
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\cdot\|_{H^{1}}
. Meanwhile, (
2.48
) implies that
(1+\tilde{q}_{k})^{-\gamma-1}
converges to
(1+\tilde{q})^{-\gamma-1}
in
L^{\infty}_{\xi}
. By equation (
2.6
), we see that
\tilde{u}_{k}
,
\tilde{q}_{k}
satisfy

\partial_{t}\tilde{u}_{k}=c_{0}^{2}(1+\tilde{q}_{k-1})^{-\gamma-1}\frac{\tilde{r}_{k-1}^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\partial_{\xi}\tilde{q}_{k}.

Noting that
\tilde{u}_{k}
converges to
\tilde{u}
in
L^{\infty}
, passing
k
to infinity and using (
2.48
)(
2.49
) yields (
1.10
). Next, (
2.5
) is equivalent to

\partial_{t}\tilde{q}_{k}=\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}(\tilde{r}_{k-1}^{2}\tilde{u}_{k})=\xi^{-2}\tilde{r}_{k-1}^{2}\partial_{\xi}\tilde{u}_{k}+2\xi^{-2}\tilde{r}_{k-1}\partial_{\xi}\tilde{r}_{k-1}\tilde{u}_{k}.

Passing
k
to infinity and using (
2.47
)(
2.50
) yields (
1.9
). For boundary conditions, passing
k
to infinity in the equation

(\tilde{u}_{k},\tilde{q}_{k})|_{\xi=1}=(u_{k},q_{k})|_{x=0}=(f^{\prime}(R_{k-1})\frac{d}{dt}f(R_{k}),f(R_{k}))

and applying the trace theorem yields
(u,q)|_{x=0}=(\tilde{u},\tilde{q})|_{\xi=1}=(\frac{dR}{dt},f(R))
, which is (
1.11
)(
1.12
). Now that
\frac{dR}{dt}=u|_{x=0}=\partial_{t}r|_{x=0}
, we recover
r|_{x=0}=R
. Moreover, (
1.9
)(
1.10
) gives
\partial_{t}\partial_{x}r^{3}=3\partial_{x}(r^{2}u)=3\partial_{t}q
and thus

\partial_{x}r^{3}-3q=\partial_{x}r_{in}^{3}-3q_{in}=3x.

Together with
r|_{x=0}=R
, this implies (
1.13
). At last, (
1.14
) holds since
\tilde{u}_{k},\,\tilde{q}_{k},\,f(R_{k})
converge to

\tilde{u},\,\tilde{q},\,f(R)
in
C\left([0,T];H^{1}_{\xi}(1,M)\right)
or
C^{1}
as
k\rightarrow+\infty
and
(\tilde{u}_{k},\,\tilde{q}_{k},\,f(R_{k}))|_{t=0}=(\tilde{u}_{in},\,\tilde{q}_{in},\,f(R_{in}))
. Therefore, we conclude that
(u,\,q,\,R)
is a solution to (
1.9
-
1.14
) with the desired regularity of Theorem 1.2. In routine, the uniqueness can be shown in a standard way by taking the difference.

Proposition 2.12

(Uniqueness)
.

Let
(u_{i},\,q_{,}\,R_{i})
,
i=1,2
be two solutions to (
1.9
-
1.14
) with
u_{i},q_{i}\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T];H^{2}\right)\cap C\left([0,T];H^{1}\right)
,
R_{i}\in C^{1}([0,T])
and
-1<\underline{q}\leq q_{i}\leq\overline{q}<+\infty
,
0<R_{i}<\overline{R}
, then
(u_{1},\,q_{1},\,R_{1})=(u_{2},\,q_{2},\,R_{2})
.

Proof.

By taking difference, we obtain

\partial_{t}(u_{1}-u_{2})=c_{0}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}r_{1}^{2}\partial_{x}(q_{1}-q_{2})+c_{0}^{2}[(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}r_{1}^{2}-(1+q_{2})^{-\gamma-1}r_{2}^{2}]\partial_{x}q_{2},

\partial_{t}(q_{1}-q_{2})=\partial_{x}[r_{1}^{2}(u_{1}-u_{2})+(r_{1}^{2}-r_{2}^{2})u_{2}],

\frac{d}{dt}(f(R_{1})-f(R_{2}))=f^{\prime}(R_{1})(u_{1}-u_{2})|_{x=0}+(f^{\prime}(R_{1})-f^{\prime}(R_{2}))u_{2}|_{x=0},

(q_{1}-q_{2})|_{x=0}=f(R_{1})-f(R_{2}).

Multiplying the first two equations by
(u_{1}-u_{2})
and
(q_{1}-q_{2})
respectively and integrating by parts yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\left((u_{1}-u_{2})^{2}+c_{0}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}(q_{1}-q_{2})^{2}\right)dx\right]

(2.51)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left[c_{0}^{2}R_{1}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}|_{x=0}(f^{\prime}(R_{1}))^{-1}(f(R_{1})-f(R_{2}))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}(q_{1}-q_{2})^{2}-c_{0}^{2}r_{1}^{2}\partial_{x}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}(u_{1}-u_{2})(q_{1}-q_{2})\right]dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}c_{0}^{2}[(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}r_{1}^{2}-(1+q_{2})^{-\gamma-1}r_{2}^{2}]\partial_{x}q_{2}(u_{1}-u_{2})dx

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}c_{0}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}\partial_{x}((r_{1}^{2}-r_{2}^{2})u_{2})(q_{1}-q_{2})dx

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}\left[(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}|_{x=0}R_{1}^{2}(f^{\prime}(R_{1})^{-1})\right](f(R_{1})-f(R_{2}))^{2}

\displaystyle+c_{0}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}|_{x=0}R_{1}^{2}(f^{\prime}(R_{1}))^{-1}\left(f^{\prime}(R_{1})-f^{\prime}(R_{2})\right)u_{2}|_{x=0}(f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})).

To control the right-hand side, write

\displaystyle\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}-1=

\displaystyle\frac{r_{2}^{3}-r_{1}^{3}}{r_{1}(r_{2}^{2}+r_{1}r_{2}+r_{1}^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[r_{1}(r_{2}^{2}+r_{2}r_{1}+r_{1}^{2}\right]^{-1}\left[R_{2}^{3}-R_{1}^{3}+\int_{0}^{x}(q_{2}-q_{1})(y,t)dy\right].

From the
L^{2}-L^{2}
boundedness of the maximal functional, it follows

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}-1\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C|f(R_{2})-f(R_{1})|+\left\|\frac{x}{r_{1}(r_{2}^{2}+r_{2}r_{1}+r_{2}^{2})}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\frac{1}{x}\int_{0}^{x}(q_{2}-q_{1})(y,t)dy\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(|f(R_{2})-f(R_{1})|+\|q_{2}-q_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

and similarly

\left\|\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}-1\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(|f(R_{2})-f(R_{1})|+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

The first term is bounded by multiple of
\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{L^{2}}
since
\partial_{t}q_{1}=\partial_{x}(r_{1}^{2}u_{1})
and
\|\partial_{x}(r_{1}^{2}u_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|r_{1}^{2}\partial_{x}q_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|u_{1}\|_{H^{2}}+\|q_{1}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C.

For the second term on the right, we compute

\displaystyle(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{r_{2}^{2}}-(1+q_{2})^{-\gamma-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(q_{1}-q_{2})\frac{(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}-(1+q_{2})^{-\gamma-1}}{q_{1}-q_{2}}+(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}-1)(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}+1),

and thus by Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\left\|(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{r_{2}^{2}}-(1+q_{2})^{-\gamma-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(|(f(R_{1})-f(R_{2}))|+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}c_{0}^{2}[(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}r_{1}^{2}-(1+q_{2})^{-\gamma-1}r_{2}^{2}]\partial_{x}q_{2}(u_{1}-u_{2})dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|r_{2}^{2}\partial_{x}q_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(|f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})|+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(|f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})|+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{L^{2}}.

For the third term, it holds

\displaystyle\partial_{x}[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{2}^{2})u_{2}]=2r_{1}^{-1}(q_{1}-q_{2})u_{2}+2(r_{1}^{-1}-r_{2}^{-1})(1+q_{2})u_{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{r_{2}^{2}}\right)r_{2}^{2}\partial_{x}u_{2},

and thus

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{x}[(r_{1}^{2}-r_{2}^{2})u_{2}]\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\frac{u_{2}}{r_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}}(1+\|q_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}})+\left(1+\|r_{2}^{-1}r_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|r_{2}^{2}\partial_{x}u_{2}\|_{L\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(|f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})|+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(|f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})|+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Since
q_{1}|_{x=0}=f(R_{1})
is a
C^{1}
function and
f
is invertible, the last two boundary terms can be controlled by multiples of
|f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})|^{2}
. Therefore, we conclude from (
2.51
) that

\frac{d}{dt}e\leq C\left(\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|q_{1}-q_{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|f(R_{1})-f(R_{2})|^{2}\right)\leq Ce

where we denote

\displaystyle e:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left((u_{1}-u_{2})^{2}+c_{0}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}(q_{1}-q_{2})^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle+c_{0}^{2}R_{1}^{2}(1+q_{1})^{-\gamma-1}|_{x=0}(f^{\prime}(R_{1}))^{-1}(f(R_{1})-f(R_{2}))^{2}.

Gronwall’s inequality then gives
e=0
on
[0,T]
, which shows
(u_{1},\,q_{1},\,R_{1})=(u_{2},\,q_{2},\,R_{2})
.
∎

3
Setup of Bootstrap

The following several sections are devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.3
. The strategy is to use a bootstrap argument encompassing the norms in energy spaces and parameters. In this section, we detail the set-up of the bootstrap.

We inherit the notations from Section
2
and drop the subscript
k
:

\displaystyle e^{j}(t):=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{j}u(t)\right)^{2}+c^{2}(t)\left(\partial_{t}^{j}q(t)\right)^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}c(t)|_{x=0}^{2}f^{\prime}(R(t))^{-1}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}f(R)(t)\right)^{2},

E(t):=\sum_{j=0}^{2}|\partial_{t}^{j}f(R)(t)|^{2}+\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 2}\left(\|L^{j_{2}}\partial_{t}^{j_{1}}q(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\tilde{L}^{j_{2}}\partial_{t}^{j_{1}}u(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

L^{1}q=c^{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}q,\,L^{2}q=\partial_{x}(c^{2}r^{4}\partial_{x}q),\,\tilde{L}^{1}u=\partial_{x}(r^{2}u),\,\tilde{L}^{2}u=c^{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}^{2}(r^{2}u).

Assume the initial value
(u_{in},\,q_{in},\,R_{in})
satisfies the assumption of Theorem
1.3
:

\epsilon^{2}:=\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|L_{0}^{j}q_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\tilde{L}_{0}^{j}u_{in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|f(R_{in})|^{2}\leq\epsilon_{0}^{2},

where the constant
\epsilon_{0}^{2}
will be determined later. Taking
r=\xi(x)=(1+3x)^{\frac{1}{3}}
in Lemma
2.2
yields
\|q_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon
, and by choosing
\epsilon_{0}
small, it holds

-\frac{1}{4}<-C\epsilon\leq q_{in}\leq C\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Meanwhile, since
f
is smooth and strictly increasing near
R=1
, we have
|R_{in}-1|\leq C\epsilon
and thus
0<1-C\epsilon\leq R_{in}\leq 1+C\epsilon<\overline{R}
. Then Theorem
1.2
gives the existence of a local solution. Note that the inequality (
2.18
) is essentially independent of
k
, therefore
\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(0)\leq\tilde{B}\epsilon^{2}
for some constant
\tilde{B}
. We now consider the time interval
[0,T]
such that the following bounds hold on
[0,T]
:

\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(t)\leq 4\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(0)\leq 4\tilde{B}\epsilon^{2},

(3.1)

-\frac{1}{2}\leq q(x,t)\leq\frac{1}{2},\;x>0,

(3.2)

and consequently for
x>0
,
t\in[0,T]

-\frac{1}{2}\leq f(R)\leq\frac{1}{2},

\frac{Ca\gamma}{2}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{\gamma+1}\leq c^{2}(x,t)=\frac{Ca\gamma}{2}(1+q(x,t))^{-\gamma-1}\leq\frac{Ca\gamma}{2}2^{\gamma+1},

R^{3}(t)+\frac{3}{2}x\leq r^{3}(x,t)=R^{3}(t)+3\int_{0}^{x}(1+q(y,t))dy\leq R^{3}(t)+\frac{9}{2}x.

Such an interval exists due to continuity and by choosing
\epsilon_{0}
further small.
By dropping the subscript in (
2.20
)(
2.21
), we obtain

\partial_{t}^{2}q-\tilde{L}^{1}\partial_{t}u=2r^{-1}u\partial_{t}q+2r^{2}\partial_{x}(r^{-1}u)u=4r^{-1}u\partial_{t}q-6r^{-2}(1+q)u^{2},

\partial_{t}^{2}u-L^{1}\partial_{t}q=\left(-(\gamma+1)(1+q)^{-1}\partial_{t}q+2r^{-1}u\right)\partial_{t}u.

Hence by Lemma
2.2
the following bounds hold on
[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\|L^{2}q\|_{L^{2}}=\|\tilde{L}^{1}\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}q\|_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tilde{L}^{1}u\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\|\partial_{t}q\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(3.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}q\|_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}q\|_{L^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{L}^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}=\|L^{1}\partial_{t}q\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}+C\left(\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tilde{L}^{1}\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}q\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(3.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}q\|_{L^{2}}+C\epsilon\left(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}q\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we have used that
\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(t)\leq 4\tilde{B}\epsilon^{2}
on
[0,T]
and
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}\lesssim 1
. Therefore, there exists a constant
A>0
such that on
[0,T]

E(t)\leq A\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(t).

(3.5)

Moreover, applying Lemma
2.2
again gives that for
x>0
,
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle|q(x,t)|+|\partial_{\xi}q(x,t)|+|\partial_{t}q(x,t)|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1},

(3.6)

\displaystyle|u(x,t)|+|\partial_{\xi}u(x,t)|+|\partial_{t}u(x,t)|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1},

where we used that
\xi^{-1}\lesssim r^{-1}\lesssim\xi^{-1}
since
R(t)
is bounded from below and above, and consequently

\displaystyle|c(x,t)-c_{0}|+|\partial_{\xi}c(x,t

\displaystyle)|+|\partial_{t}c(x,t)|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1},

(3.7)

\displaystyle|R-

\displaystyle 1|\lesssim\epsilon.

By Hölder and (
1.13
), we also compute

\displaystyle\left|\frac{r(x,t)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}-1\right|=

\displaystyle\frac{\xi r(x,t)+\xi^{2}}{r(x,t)^{2}+\xi r(x,t)+\xi^{2}}\xi^{-3}\left|R(t)^{3}-1+3\int_{0}^{x}q(y,t)dy\right|

(3.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\xi^{-3}\left(|R(t)^{3}-1|+x^{\frac{1}{2}}\|q\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\xi^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Combining (
3.7
)(
3.8
) yields

\left|cr^{2}\xi^{-2}-c_{0}\right|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1},

(3.9)

and thus there exist constants
\overline{c}
and
\underline{c}
such that

-\epsilon\lesssim\underline{c}-c_{0}\leq cr^{2}\xi^{-2}-c_{0}\leq\overline{c}-c_{0}\lesssim\epsilon.

(3.10)

If
T
is large enough so that
1+\overline{c}T\geq\exp\left(\frac{\kappa_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)
, then we have obtained the almost global existence. Hence we assume without loss of generality that

1+\overline{c}T\leq\exp\left(\frac{\kappa}{\epsilon}\right)

(3.11)

holds for a constant
\kappa\leq\kappa_{0}
with
\kappa_{0}
to be determined.
The heart of the proof to the almost global existence is the following:

Proposition 3.13
.

Assume that (
3.1
)(
3.2
) and consequently (
3.3
-
3.10
) hold on
[0,T]
with
T
satisfying (
3.11
). Then the following holds:

1. The bounds (
3.1
)(
3.2
) can be strictly improved on
[0,T]
. To be specific, we will prove

\sum_{j=0}^{2}e^{j}(t)\leq 3\tilde{B}\epsilon^{2},\;t\in[0,T],

(3.12)

and thus
|q(x,t)|\lesssim\epsilon
by (
3.6
), which improves (
3.2
) in turn for suitable
\epsilon_{0}
.

2. (Control of error). The following estimate holds on
[0,T]
:

\left|F(R)-\mathcal{R}(t)\right|\lesssim\frac{\log(1+\overline{c}t)}{1+\underline{c}t}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\tilde{\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}}{1+\underline{c}t}\left(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+\tilde{\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)+\frac{\epsilon}{(1+\underline{c}t)^{2}}(\epsilon+\tilde{\epsilon}).

Remark 3.14
.

In fact, system (
1.4
-
1.8
) admits a conserved energy. We have by (
1.4
) and (
1.5
) that

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle u\left(\partial_{t}u+\frac{Ca}{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}(\rho^{\gamma})\right)+\frac{Ca}{2}\rho^{-2}\left(\rho^{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\partial_{t}\rho+\rho^{2}\partial_{x}(r^{2}u)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+\frac{Ca}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1}-\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}+\rho^{-1}\right)\right)+\frac{Ca}{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{2}u\left(\rho^{\gamma}-1\right)\right).

Integrating the above identity on the strip
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,T]
and using the boundary conditions gives

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+H(\rho)\right)(T)dx-\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+H(\rho)\right)(0)dx

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\left(R^{2}\left(\left(\frac{Ca}{2}+\frac{2}{We}\right)R^{-3\gamma_{0}}-\frac{2}{We}R^{-1}-\frac{Ca}{2}\right)\frac{dR}{dt}\right)dt,

where
H(\rho):=\frac{Ca}{2(\gamma-1)}\left(\rho^{\gamma-1}-\gamma+(\gamma-1)\rho^{-1}\right)
denotes the entropy. Note that

\displaystyle-R^{2}\left(\left(\frac{Ca}{2}+\frac{2}{We}\right)R^{-3\gamma_{0}}-\frac{2}{We}R^{-1}-\frac{Ca}{2}\right)\frac{dR}{dt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\{\frac{1}{3\gamma_{0}-3}\left(\frac{Ca}{2}+\frac{2}{We}\right)\left(R^{-3\gamma_{0}+3}-1\right)+\frac{1}{We}\left(R^{2}-1\right)+\frac{Ca}{6}\left(R^{3}-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}W(R).

W(R)
is actually the work done by the surface tension and the pressure at both the bubble surface and the infinity. Since
W(R)
is convex in
R
, we obtain the conserved energy:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+H(\rho)\right)dx+W(R).

This conserved energy can give a uniform in time the upper and lower bound of
R
as well as the control of
L^{2}
norms of
u
and
q
. However, since
L^{2}
is subcritical compared with the existence space, such controls are of less importance, and we will not use this in the proceeding proof.

4
KSS type estimate with boundary terms

In this section, we establish a KSS (Keel-Smith-Sogge) type estimate regarding the quasilinear wave equation (
1.18
). This will provide a long-time control over the weighted norms of the solution, which will be used later to improve (
3.1
). However, due to the non-vanishing boundary value, we will obtain boundary terms in this KSS type estimate. Nevertheless, these boundary terms have positive signs in higher derivatives and negative signs in lower derivatives. Therefore, by taking a delicate linear combination, the only left bad term is the one with the lowest regularity, whose treatment will be given in the next two sections. To be specific, the goal of this section is to prove the estimate below.

Proposition 4.15
.

Let
\varphi
be a solution to (
1.18
) on
[0,T]
, and assume that (
3.1
)(
3.2
) hold on
[0,T]
, then the following bound holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right.

(4.1)

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{c}{\rho r}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{\log_{2}T,1\right\}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx+\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{2}|_{x=0}dt.

Proof.

Step 1.
We begin with defining a sequence of modified momentum density for
j=0,1,2
and
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
:

P_{0,k}^{j}:=M_{k}\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{1}{2}N_{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi,

P_{1,k}^{j}:=\frac{1}{2}M_{k}\left[\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{2}N_{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi-\frac{1}{4}cr^{2}(\partial_{x}N_{k})(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi)^{2},

where
M_{k}
,
N_{k}
are undetermined weight functions.
By taking time derivative in (
1.18
), the following holds:

\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi-c^{2}\partial_{x}(r^{4}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi)=[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi.

(4.2)

We compute by brute force and (
4.2
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(-\partial_{t}P_{0,k}^{j}+\partial_{x}(cr^{2}P_{1,k}^{j})\right)dxdt

(4.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{r^{2}\partial_{x}(cM_{k})}{2}+\frac{c\partial_{x}r^{2}M_{k}}{2}-\frac{N_{k}}{2}\right)\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{r^{2}\partial_{x}(cM_{k})}{2}+\frac{N_{k}}{2}-\frac{c\partial_{x}r^{2}M_{k}}{2}\right)\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{x}(c^{2}r^{4}\partial_{x}N_{k})\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle+\left(\text{nonlinear terms}\right),

with the nonlinearities given by

\displaystyle\left(\text{nonlinear terms}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}M_{k}(r^{2}\partial_{x}c)\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}M_{k}\frac{\partial_{t}(cr^{2})}{cr^{2}}\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}N_{k}(r^{2}\partial_{x}c)\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi dxdt

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(M_{k}cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{1}{2}N_{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi dxdt

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{t}M_{k}\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi dxdt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial_{t}N_{k}}{2}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi dxdt.

The construction of
P_{0}
’s and
P_{1}
’s as well as the calculation is an analogue to Section 5 of
[
34
]
, which also considers a more general case without symmetry; see also
[
19
]
, but for integrity, we write down the computation process of the present context in Appendix A.
Now select
M_{k}:=\frac{1}{c}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}
,
N_{k}:=c\partial_{x}r^{2}\cdot M_{k}=\frac{2}{\rho r}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}
, and thus the coefficients of the leading-order quadratic terms are

\frac{r^{2}\partial_{x}(cM_{k})}{2}+\frac{c\partial_{x}r^{2}M_{k}}{2}-\frac{N_{k}}{2}=\frac{r^{2}\partial_{x}(cM_{k})}{2}-\frac{c\partial_{x}r^{2}M_{k}}{2}+\frac{N_{k}}{2}=\frac{r^{2}}{2\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}.

To compute the coefficient of the third quadratic term, we have

-\partial_{x}\left(c^{2}r^{4}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{1}{\rho r}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)\right)=-\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right)+\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)+\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right),

-\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right)=-\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}-\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{5}}\frac{2^{k}(2^{k}-\xi)}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}},

\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)=\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}},

\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)=\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}.

Hence the coefficient of the third term is

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{x}\left(c^{2}r^{4}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{2}{\rho r}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\frac{\xi-2^{k}}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}}{\xi(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}.

The principal part is
\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}}{\xi(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}
, while the terms left will be regarded as nonlinearities.
Next, by divergence theorem, there is

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,0)dx-\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,T)dx-\int_{0}^{T}\left(cr^{2}P_{1,k}^{j}\right)(0,t)dt

(4.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(-\partial_{t}P_{0,k}^{j}+\partial_{x}(cr^{2}P_{1,k}^{j})\right)dxdt.

Moreover, we expand the boundary term
P_{1,k}^{j}(0,t)
as

\displaystyle P_{1,k}^{j}(0,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\left[\frac{1}{2}M_{k}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}N_{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right]\right|_{x=0}

\displaystyle-\left.\left[\frac{1}{4}cr^{2}\partial_{x}N_{k}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]\right|_{x=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\left[\frac{1}{2}M_{k}\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{k}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{N_{k}}{2M_{k}}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]\right|_{x=0}

\displaystyle-\left.\left[\left(\frac{N_{k}^{2}}{8M_{k}}+\frac{1}{4}cr^{2}\partial_{x}N_{k}\right)\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]\right|_{x=0},

and compute the coefficients explicitly:

\frac{1}{2}M_{k}(0,t)=\frac{1}{2c(0,t)}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1},\quad\frac{N_{k}(0,t)}{2M_{k}(0,t)}=\left(\frac{c}{\rho r}\right)(0,t),

\frac{N_{k}(0,t)^{2}}{8M_{k}(0,t)}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{c}{\rho^{2}r^{2}}(0,t)\right)\frac{1}{2^{k}+1},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}N_{k}\right)(0,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\frac{1}{4}\left[cr^{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{2}{\rho r}\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right)\right]\right|_{x=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{cr}{\rho}\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right]\right|_{x=0}-\left.\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}\left(r\partial_{x}\rho+r^{-2}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right]\right|_{x=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left.\left(\frac{cr}{\rho}\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+1)^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\left.\left(\frac{c}{\rho^{2}r^{2}}\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}-\frac{1}{2}\left.\left(\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}r\partial_{x}\rho\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1},

\frac{N_{k}(0,t)^{2}}{8M_{k}(0,t)}+\frac{1}{4}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}N_{k}\right)(0,t)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left.\frac{cr}{\rho}\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+1)^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\left.\left(\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}r\partial_{x}\rho\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}.

Combining (
4.3
)(
4.4
) yields the identity

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{2\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

(4.5)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{c^{2}}{2\rho^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}}{\xi(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\frac{R^{2}}{2}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\frac{R^{2}}{2}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}\left.\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{c}{\rho r}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,0)dx-\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,T)dx+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho}\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+1)^{2}}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{8}NL_{k,l}^{j},

with the nonlinearities given by

\displaystyle NL_{k,1}^{j}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right]\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,2}^{j}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}(r^{2}\partial_{x}c)M_{k}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,3}^{j}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}M_{k}\frac{\partial_{t}(cr^{2})}{cr^{2}}\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,4}^{j}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}N_{k}(r^{2}\partial_{x}c)\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,5}^{j}:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(M_{k}cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{1}{2}N_{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,6}^{j}:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{t}M_{k}\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\cdot cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,7}^{j}:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial_{t}N_{k}}{2}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi dxdt,

\displaystyle NL_{k,8}^{j}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\left.\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\partial_{x}\rho\right)\right|_{x=0}\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt.

Note that
\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\simeq\frac{1}{\xi}
and
\frac{2^{k}}{\xi(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}\simeq\frac{1}{\xi^{3}}
for
\xi\in[2^{k},2^{k+1}]
. Summing in
k
with
2^{k}\leq T
, or equivalently, for
k\leq K
with
K:=\max\{-1,\lfloor\log_{2}T\rfloor\}
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

(4.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{2^{k}\leq\xi<2^{k+1}}\frac{1}{\xi}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\xi\geq 2^{K+1}}\frac{1}{\xi}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{2\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

\displaystyle+\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx,

where we used
2^{-(K+1)}\leq T^{-1}
and (
3.2
), and similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{2^{k}\leq\xi<2^{k+1}}\frac{1}{\xi^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\xi\geq 2^{K+1}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}}{\xi(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dx.

Step 2.
In this part, we shall bound the nonlinearities by the left hand of (
4.6
) and (
4.7
) with the help of (
3.1
-
3.10
). Since we are using the new unknown
\varphi
, it is necessary to build a bridge between
\varphi
and
u
,
\rho
. Recall
\frac{c}{\rho}u=cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi
and (
1.17
) that
\partial_{t}\varphi+\frac{Ca\gamma}{2(\gamma-1)}(\rho^{\gamma-1}-1)=\frac{1}{2}u^{2}
. By taking time derivatives, the following equations hold:

\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi-\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\partial_{t}q=u\partial_{t}u,

\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi-\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\partial_{t}^{2}q=\partial_{t}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\right)\partial_{t}q+\left(\partial_{t}u\right)^{2}+u\partial_{t}^{2}u.

Similarly, taking time derivatives on
\partial_{x}\varphi=\frac{u}{\rho r^{2}}
yields

cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\varphi=\frac{c}{\rho}\partial_{t}u+cu\left(\partial_{t}q-\frac{2u}{\rho r}\right),

cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi=\frac{c}{\rho}\partial_{t}^{2}u+2c\left(\partial_{t}q-\frac{2u}{\rho r}\right)\partial_{t}u+cu\left(-\frac{2}{\rho}\frac{\partial_{t}u}{r}+\frac{6u^{2}}{\rho r^{2}}-\frac{4u\partial_{t}q}{r}+\partial_{t}^{2}q\right).

Hence by (
3.6
)(
3.7
)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}\varphi-\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\left(\rho^{\gamma-1}-1\right)\right|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}|u|\simeq

\displaystyle\epsilon\xi^{-1}|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi-\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\partial_{t}q\right|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}|u|\simeq\epsilon\xi^{-1}

\displaystyle|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi-\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\partial_{t}^{2}q\right|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}(|\partial_{t}q|+|\partial_{t}

\displaystyle u|+|\partial_{t}^{2}u|),

\displaystyle|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\varphi-\frac{c}{\rho}\partial_{t}u|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}|u|\simeq\epsilon\xi^{-1}|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|

\displaystyle,

(4.9)

\displaystyle|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi-\frac{c}{\rho}\partial_{t}^{2}u|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}(|u|+|\partial_{t}u|+|\partial_{t}^{2}q|

\displaystyle).

Moreover, for sufficiently small
\epsilon_{0}
and
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
, (
4.8
)(
4.9
) further implies that

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{2}u|+|\partial_{t}^{2}q|\lesssim

\displaystyle|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|+|\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi|+\epsilon\xi^{-1}(|u|+|\partial_{t}q|+|\partial_{t}u|)

(4.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|+|\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi|+\epsilon\xi^{-1}(|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|+|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\varphi|+|\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|),

and consequently

|\partial_{t}^{2}u-\rho r^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left(|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi|+|\partial_{t}^{j+1}\varphi|\right),

(4.11)

|\partial_{t}^{2}q-\frac{\rho}{c^{2}}\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left(|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi|+|\partial_{t}^{j+1}\varphi|\right).

(4.12)

\bullet

NL_{1,k}^{j}
terms.

We use the bootstrap bounds (
3.6
)(
3.8
) to compute

\left|\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}\right|\lesssim\left(|\rho-1|+\rho\left|\frac{r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}-1\right|\right)\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}\lesssim\epsilon\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{3}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}},

\left|\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right|\lesssim\left|\partial_{x}\left(\rho^{\gamma}\frac{r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\right|\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}},

\left|\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right|\lesssim|\partial_{x}(\rho^{\gamma-1})|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\lesssim\epsilon\frac{1}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)},

\left|\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right|=\left|c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}q\right|\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}.

Noting that
L^{2}q=\partial_{x}\left(c^{2}r^{4}\partial_{x}q\right)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x}(c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}q)\right|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\leq\left|\frac{c^{2}}{\rho}\partial_{x}q\right|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{r}|L^{2}q|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)}+|L^{2}q|\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}.

Using the above bounds, we estimate
NL_{1,k}^{j}
except the term involving
\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\rho r^{3}}{\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}}{\rho\xi^{3}}\right)\frac{2^{k}\xi}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{2^{k}}{(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\right]\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2^{2k}}{\xi^{3}(2^{k}+\xi)^{3}}+\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}+\frac{1}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)}\right)\left|\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon(1+K)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left|\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right|^{2}dxdt,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left|\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right|^{2}dxdt.

The last inequality holds due to the assumption (
3.11
) and that

\epsilon(1+K)\leq\epsilon\max\{0,1+\lfloor\log_{2}T\rfloor\}\lesssim\kappa.

(4.13)

Next, we integrate by parts and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\left(c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}q\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)|_{x=0}dt\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}cr^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}q\left(\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\frac{2\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)dxdt\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2^{k}}{\xi^{2}(2^{k}+\xi)^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right)

\displaystyle+\epsilon\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt.

Then sum in
k
and use (
4.13
) again:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\right)\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\xi^{-1}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]dxdt+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt.

Therefore, we conclude with the control of
NL_{1,k}^{j}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{1,k}^{j}\right|

(4.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\xi^{-1}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]dxdt+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt.

\bullet

NL_{2,k}^{j}
,
NL_{3,k}^{j}
,
NL_{4,k}^{j}
,
NL_{8,k}^{j}
terms
. These terms can be bound easily by using (
3.6
)(
3.7
) and (
4.13
):

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{2,k}^{j}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{r^{2}\partial_{x}c}{c}\right|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

(4.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{3,k}^{j}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{\partial_{t}(cr^{2})}{c^{2}r^{2}}\right|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

(4.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{4,k}^{j}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2}{\rho r}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}|r^{2}\partial_{x}c|\left(\xi^{-2}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt,

(4.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}\left(\xi^{-2}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{8,k}^{j}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\left.\left|c^{2}r^{3}\partial_{x}q\right|_{x=0}\right|\frac{1}{2^{k}+1}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt

(4.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt.

\bullet

NL_{5,k}^{j}
term.
We expand the commutators and use (
1.18
) to obtain:

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}]\left(\partial_{x}(r^{4}\partial_{x}\varphi)\right)+c^{2}\partial_{x}\left([\partial_{t}^{j},r^{4}]\partial_{x}\varphi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}]\left(c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi\right)+c^{2}\partial_{x}\left([\partial_{t}^{j},r^{4}]r^{-4}r^{4}\partial_{x}\varphi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{j},c^{2}]\left(c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi\right)+c^{2}[\partial_{t}^{j},r^{4}]r^{-4}\left(c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi\right)+c^{2}\left[\partial_{x},[\partial_{t}^{j},r^{4}]r^{-4}\right]r^{4}\partial_{x}\varphi.

For
j=1
, we compute:

\displaystyle[\partial_{t},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi=\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c^{2}}{c^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi+c^{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)r^{4}\partial_{x}\varphi

and using (
3.6
):

\left|[\partial_{t},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi\right|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi\right|+\epsilon\xi^{-2}\left|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi\right|.

For
j=2
, the commutator reads

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{2},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}^{2}c^{2}+2\partial_{t}c^{2}\partial_{t}\right)\left(c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi\right)+c^{2}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}r^{4}}{r^{4}}+2\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\partial_{t}+2\partial_{t}r^{4}\partial_{t}r^{-4}\right)\left(c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi\right)

\displaystyle+c^{2}\left[\partial_{x},\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}r^{4}}{r^{4}}+2\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\partial_{t}+2\partial_{t}r^{4}\partial_{t}r^{-4}\right]r^{4}\partial_{x}\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(2\frac{\partial_{t}c^{2}}{c^{2}}+2\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}c^{2}}{c^{2}}+2\partial_{t}c^{2}\partial_{t}c^{-2}+\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}r^{4}}{r^{4}}+2\partial_{t}r^{4}\partial_{t}r^{-4}+2\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}c^{2}\partial_{t}c^{-2}\right)\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi

\displaystyle+c^{2}r^{2}\left(\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)+2\partial_{x}\left(\partial_{t}r^{4}\partial_{t}r^{-4}\right)\right)r^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi

\displaystyle+2c^{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)\left(r^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\varphi+\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}r^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi\right).

We also compute:

\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}c^{2}}{c^{2}}=-\left(\gamma+1\right)\frac{\partial_{t}(c^{2}\rho\partial_{t}q)}{c^{2}}=-\left(\gamma+1\right)\rho\partial_{t}^{2}q+\left(\gamma+1\right)\left(\gamma+2\right)c^{2}\rho^{2}(\partial_{t}q)^{2},

\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}r^{4}}{r^{4}}=4\frac{\partial_{t}u}{r}+12\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}},

\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)=4\partial_{x}\left(\frac{u}{r}\right)=4r^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}q-3\rho^{-1}u\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}^{2}r^{4}}{r^{4}}\right)=

\displaystyle 4\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}(r^{2}u)}{r^{3}}+\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4r^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{2}q-\frac{3}{\rho r^{3}}\left(2ru^{2}+r^{2}\partial_{t}u\right)-\frac{6u^{2}}{\rho r^{2}}+\frac{2u}{r}\partial_{t}q\right),

where we used that
\partial_{t}\partial_{x}(r^{2}u)=\partial_{t}^{2}q
to avoid the presence of mixed derivative.
Therefore, it follows from (
3.6
)(
3.7
) that

\displaystyle\left|[\partial_{t}^{2},c^{2}\partial_{x}r^{4}\partial_{x}]\varphi\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\xi^{-1}|\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi|+\epsilon\xi^{-2}\left(|\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|+|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\varphi|+\xi^{-1}|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|\right)

\displaystyle+|\partial_{t}^{2}q|\left(|\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|+\xi^{-1}|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|\right).

Then we conclude in view of (
4.8
)(
4.10
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left(\left|NL_{5,k}^{1}\right|+\left|NL_{5,k}^{2}\right|\right)\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{i}\varphi|+\xi^{-1}|\partial_{t}^{i}\varphi|\right)

(4.19)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(|\partial_{t}^{3}\varphi|+|\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi|+|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}\varphi|+|cr^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi|\right)dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt.

\bullet

NL_{6,k}^{j}
,
NL_{7,k}^{j}
terms.

We estimate by (
3.6
)(
3.7
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{6,k}^{j}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\partial_{t}c^{-1}\right|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}|\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi||cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi|dxdt

(4.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}|\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi||cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi|dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|NL_{7,k}^{j}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\rho r}\right)\right|\frac{\xi}{2^{k}+\xi}|\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi||\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi|dxdt

(4.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\frac{1}{2^{k}+\xi}|\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi||\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi|dxdt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right).

Step 3.
Now we combine (
4.5
)(
4.6
)(
4.7
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{c}{\rho r}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left\{\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,0)dx\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,T)dx\right|+\sum_{l=1}^{8}\left|NL_{l,k}^{j}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt+\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\xi^{-2}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx.

By the explicit expression of
P_{0,k}^{j}
and Hardy’s inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}P_{0,k}^{j}(x,t)dx\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+K)\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\xi^{-2}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{\log_{2}T,1\right\}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx.

Moreover, by taking a suitable linear combination of (
4.22
) for
j=0,1,2
, we can cancel the boundary term on the right-hand side except the one with the lowest order. Then we conclude by using the bounds (
4.14
-
4.21
) of nonlinearities:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-1}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}dxdt\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi+\frac{c}{\rho r}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{\log_{2}T,1\right\}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx+\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{2}|_{x=0}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{8}\left|NL_{l,k}^{j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{\log_{2}T,1\right\}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\max_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)dx+\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{2}|_{x=0}dt

\displaystyle+\kappa\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\xi^{-1}\left(\left(\partial_{t}^{1+j}\varphi\right)^{2}+\left(cr^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right)+\xi^{-3}\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right]dxdt\right\}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\sum_{j=0}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left.\left(\partial_{t}^{j}\varphi\right)^{2}\right|_{x=0}dt.

Hence for sufficiently small
\kappa_{0}
and
\epsilon_{0}
we arrive at (
4.1
).
∎

5
Derivation of precise boundary ODE

In order to handle the boundary value of
\varphi
present on the right-hand side of (
4.1
), we derive in this section the equation satisfied by
\varphi|_{x=0}
. Introduce
\psi:=r\varphi
, and substitute
\varphi
by
\frac{\psi}{r}
in (
1.18
) yields

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle r^{-1}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi+2\partial_{t}r^{-1}\partial_{t}\psi+\partial_{t}^{2}r^{-1}\psi-c^{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{3}\partial_{x}\psi+r^{4}\partial_{x}r^{-1}\psi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{-1}\left(\partial_{t}^{2}\psi+2r\partial_{t}r^{-1}\partial_{t}\psi-c^{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{2}\partial_{x}\psi\right)\right)+\left(\partial_{t}^{2}r^{-1}-c^{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{4}\partial_{x}r^{-1}\right)\right)\psi.

On the other hand, from (
1.5
) it follows

\displaystyle 0=\partial_{t}u+\frac{Ca}{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}(\rho^{\gamma})=\partial_{t}^{2}r-\frac{Ca\gamma}{2}\rho^{\gamma+1}r^{2}\partial_{x}\rho^{-1}=\partial_{t}r^{2}-c^{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}(r^{2}\partial_{x}r),

and thus

\partial_{t}^{2}r^{-1}-c^{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{4}\partial_{x}r^{-1}\right)=\partial_{t}^{2}r^{-1}+c^{2}\partial_{x}(r^{2}\partial_{x}r)=\partial_{t}^{2}r^{-1}+r^{-2}\partial_{t}^{2}r=2r^{-3}(\partial_{t}r)^{2}.

Henceforth

0=\partial_{t}^{2}\psi-c^{2}r^{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{2}\partial_{x}\psi\right)-2\frac{u}{r}\partial_{t}\psi+2\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\psi.

We rewrite the above equation to a hyperbolic system of the form

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\partial_{x}c}{c}+\frac{2u}{r}\right)cr^{2}\partial_{x}\psi-\frac{2u}{r}\partial_{t}\psi+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\left(c^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi\right)+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\partial_{x}c}{2c}\right)\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi

\displaystyle-\frac{2u}{r}\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\psi,

and similarly,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle c^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\left(c^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi\right)+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c-cr^{2}\partial_{x}c}{2c}\right)\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi

\displaystyle-\frac{2u}{r}\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\psi.

By introducing
w_{F}=\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi
and
w_{B}=\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi
, which in fact stand for the forward and backward pressure wave, we see that
w_{F}
,
w_{B}
satisfy the transport equations for
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,T]
:

0=\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\partial_{x}c}{2c}\right)\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{2u}{r}\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(5.1)

0=\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c-cr^{2}\partial_{x}c}{2c}\right)\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{2u}{r}\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(5.2)

One can check that (
5.1
)(
5.2
) is genuinely nonlinear in
w_{F}
,
w_{B}
, and this is actually a main feature of the isentropic compressible Euler equations. In view of Majda’s conjecture
[
31
, Page 89]
, it is reasonable not to expect a global solution. To solve (
5.1
)(
5.2
) as a hyperbolic system on the half line, we shall need the boundary data of
w_{F}
. Recalling (
1.7
) and taking time derivative yields

\left.\frac{Ca\gamma}{2}\rho^{\gamma-1}\partial_{t}\rho\right|_{x=0}=\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\left.\left(\rho r^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi\right)\right|_{x=0}=\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\left.\left(\rho r\partial_{x}\psi-r^{-2}\psi\right)\right|_{x=0}.

Then substituting
\rho^{\gamma}-1
by (
1.17
) gives

-\left.\partial_{t}\left(\partial_{t}\varphi-\frac{1}{2}u^{2}\right)\right|_{x=0}=\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\left.\left(r\partial_{x}\psi\right)\right|_{x=0}-\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\left.\left(\rho^{-1}r^{-2}\psi\right)\right|_{x=0}.

In order to obtain an equation of
\psi|_{x=0}
, we compute

\partial_{t}^{2}\varphi=r^{-1}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi+2\partial_{t}r^{-1}\partial_{t}\psi+\partial_{t}^{2}r^{-1}\psi=r^{-1}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi-2\frac{u}{r^{2}}\partial_{t}\psi-\left(\frac{\partial_{t}u}{r^{2}}-2\frac{u^{2}}{r^{3}}\right)\psi,

r^{2}\partial_{x}\psi=\frac{1}{c}\left(w_{B}-\partial_{t}\psi\right),

u=\rho r^{2}\partial_{x}\varphi=\rho r\partial_{x}\psi-r^{-2}\psi=\frac{\rho}{rc}\left(w_{B}-\partial_{t}\psi\right)-r^{-2}\psi.

Therefore, by writing
r|_{x=0}=R
we obtain

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\left\{\partial_{t}^{2}\psi-Ru\partial_{t}u-\left(\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)}{c}+2R^{-1}u\right)\partial_{t}\psi\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\left(\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\rho^{-1}R^{-1}-2R^{-2}u^{2}+R^{-1}\partial_{t}u\right)\psi+\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)}{c}w_{B}\right\}\right|_{x=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\partial_{t}^{2}\psi-\left(\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)}{c}+2R^{-1}u-\frac{\rho\partial_{t}u}{c}\right)\partial_{t}\psi\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\left(\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\rho^{-1}R^{-1}-2R^{-2}u^{2}-R^{-1}\partial_{t}u\right)\psi+\left(\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)}{c}-\frac{\rho\partial_{t}u}{c}\right)w_{B}\right\}\right|_{x=0}.

In short, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}\psi|_{x=0}+b_{1}\partial_{t}\psi|_{x=0}+b_{0}\psi|_{x=0}+aw_{B}|_{x=0}=0,

(5.3)

with

b_{1}(t):=-\left.\left(\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)}{c}+2R^{-1}u-\frac{\rho\partial_{t}u}{c}\right)\right|_{x=0},

b_{0}(t):=\left.-\left(\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)\rho^{-1}R^{-1}-2R^{-2}u^{2}-R^{-1}\partial_{t}u\right)\right|_{x=0},

a(t):=\left.\left(\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(R)}{c}-\frac{\rho\partial_{t}u}{c}\right)\right|_{x=0}.

Moreover, we estimate these coefficients by using (
3.6
)(
3.7
) and the smoothness of
\tilde{f}
near
1
:

\displaystyle|b_{1}(t)+\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)}{c_{0}}|\lesssim\epsilon,\;|b_{0}(t)+\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)|\lesssim\epsilon,\;|a(t)-\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)}{c_{0}}|\lesssim\epsilon.

(5.4)

Let
\Lambda_{1}
,
\Lambda_{2}
denote the two eigenvalues of the unperturbed equation, namely, the two roots of
\lambda^{2}-\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)}{c_{0}}\lambda-\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)
. Then we rewrite (
5.3
) in the form of column vectors and diagonalize the resulted equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y-\left[\begin{matrix}\Lambda_{1}&\\
&\Lambda_{2}\end{matrix}\right]Y+\Delta Y+W=0,

(5.5)

Y:=\left[\begin{matrix}1&-\Lambda_{2}\\
1&-\Lambda_{1}\end{matrix}\right]\left[\begin{matrix}\partial_{t}\psi|_{x=0}\\
\psi|_{x=0}\end{matrix}\right],\;\Delta=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}}\left[\begin{matrix}\delta_{1}&\delta_{2}\\
\delta_{1}&\delta_{2}\end{matrix}\right],\;W=\left[\begin{matrix}aw_{B}|_{x=0}\\
aw_{B}|_{x=0}\end{matrix}\right],

\delta_{1}=\Lambda_{1}\left(b_{1}+\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)}{c_{0}}\right)+\left(b_{0}+\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)\right),\;\delta_{2}=-\Lambda_{2}\left(b_{1}+\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)}{c_{0}}\right)-\left(b_{0}+\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)\right).

By solving (
5.5
) it follows

\displaystyle Y(t)=

\displaystyle\exp\left[\begin{matrix}\Lambda_{1}t&\\
&\Lambda_{2}t\end{matrix}\right]Y(0)-\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left[\begin{matrix}\Lambda_{1}(t-s)&\\
&\Lambda_{2}(t-s)\end{matrix}\right]\left(\Delta Y\right)(s)ds

(5.6)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left[\begin{matrix}\Lambda_{1}(t-s)&\\
&\Lambda_{2}(t-s)\end{matrix}\right]W(s)ds.

We remark here that from (
1.7
) it holds

\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)=-3\gamma_{0}\left(\frac{Ca}{2}+\frac{2}{We}\right)+\frac{2}{We}<0
, which shows that
\text{Re}\Lambda_{i}<0
,
i=1,2
.

6
The analysis of backward pressure wave

Recall the KSS type estimate (
4.1
). To control the boundary term
\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{2}|_{x=0}dt
, we have obtained in the previous section the integral equation (
5.6
) satisfied by
\psi:=r^{-1}\varphi
with the source term containing the backward pressure wave
w_{B}
. To close this argument, we will estimate
w_{B}
in this section by using the equations (
5.1
)(
5.2
), which form a hyperbolic system on the domain
\left\{(x,t):x\geq 0,t\geq 0\right\}
. Generally, in view of the signs of characteristic speeds, we will need the data of
w_{B}
on
\{t=0\}
and the data of
w_{F}
on
\{t=0\}\cup\{x=0\}
to solve the Cauchy problem associated with system (
5.1
)(
5.2
), but in the present context, the data of
w_{F}
on
\{x=0\}
is instead given implicitly by equation (
5.6
) and the relation
w_{F}=\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi=2\partial_{t}\psi-w_{B}
.

Recall that
\xi=(1+3x)^{\frac{1}{3}}
and we rewrite (
5.1
)(
5.2
) as

0=\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}\right)\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}c}{2c}\right)\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{2u}{r}\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(6.1)

0=\left(\partial_{t}-cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}\right)\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c-cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}c}{2c}\right)\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{2u}{r}\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{2u^{2}}{r^{2}}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(6.2)

Now we begin the analysis by introducing the characteristics. Note that the bootstrap bounds (
3.6
)(
3.7
) imply the Lipschitz continuity of
cr^{2}\xi^{-2}
in both
\xi
and
t
argument. In fact, we have

\left|\partial_{\xi}(cr^{2}\xi^{-2})\right|+\left|\partial_{t}(cr^{2}\xi^{-2})\right|\lesssim\epsilon\xi^{-1}.

(6.3)

For each
\xi\in[1,+\infty)
,
t\in[0,T]
, define
X_{B}
as the unique solution (figure
1
) to

\left\{\begin{aligned} &X_{B}(\xi,t;0)=(\xi,t),\\
&\frac{d}{ds}X_{B}(\xi,t;s)=\left(-\left(cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\right)(X_{B}(\xi,t;s)),1\right),\end{aligned}\right.

and
X_{F}
to be the unique solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} &X_{F}(\xi,t;0)=(\xi,t),\\
&\frac{d}{ds}X_{F}(\xi,t;s)=\left(\left(cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\right)(X_{F}(\xi,t;s)),1\right).\end{aligned}\right.

We then obtain the integral equation corresponding to (
6.1
)(
6.2
):

\displaystyle\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;s)\right)

(6.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s}\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma\right\}\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;0)\right)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{s}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s-\sigma}\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;\tau)\right)d\tau\right\}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}c}{2c}\right)\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{s}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s-\sigma}\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;\tau)\right)d\tau\right\}2r^{-2}u^{2}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma,

\displaystyle\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;s)\right)

(6.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s}\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma\right\}\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;0)\right)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{s}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s-\sigma}\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;\tau)\right)d\tau\right\}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}c}{2c}\right)\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{s}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s-\sigma}\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;\tau)\right)d\tau\right\}2r^{-2}u^{2}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma.

For each
t\in[0,T]
, we denote
\xi_{0}(t):=X_{B}(1,t;-t)
, and thus
X_{B}(\xi_{0}(t),0;t)=(1,t)
. Meanwhile, for each
t_{*}\in[0,T]
,
denote by
\Omega_{B}(t_{*})
the backward acoustic cone

\Omega_{B}(t_{*}):=\left\{(\xi,t):0\leq t\leq t_{*},\;1\leq\xi\leq X_{B}(\xi(t_{*}),0;t)\right\}.

Introduce the new unknowns
v_{F}(\xi,t,t_{*})
,
v_{B}(\xi,t,t_{*})
by

v_{F}(\xi,t;t_{*}):=\sup\left\{\left|\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;s)\right)\right|:X_{F}(\xi,t;s)\in\Omega_{B}(t_{*})\right\},

v_{B}(\xi,t;t_{*}):=\sup\left\{\left|\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;s)\right)\right|:X_{B}(\xi,t;s)\in\Omega_{B}(t_{*})\right\}.

Figure 1:
The backward and forward characteristics within the domain
\Omega_{B}(t_{*})

X_{B}(\xi,t;s)\in\Omega_{B}(t_{*})
is automatically fulfilled for each
(\xi,t)\in\Omega_{B}(t_{*})
due to the uniqueness of integral curves. Therefore, we abbreviate
v_{B}(\xi,t;t_{*})
to
v_{B}(\xi,t)
. Moreover, from the definition we have

v_{F}\left(X_{F}(\xi,t;\tau);t_{*}\right)=v_{F}(\xi,t;t_{*}),\quad v_{B}\left(X_{B}(\xi,t;\tau)\right)=v_{B}(\xi,t).

It then follows from (
6.4
)(
6.5
) that

\displaystyle v_{F}(1,t;t_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma\right\}\left\{\left|\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}(1,t)\right|\right.

(6.6)

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}c}{2c}\right)\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)\right|v_{B}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(2r^{-2}u^{2}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma\right\},

\displaystyle v_{F}(\xi,0;t_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{F}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma\right\}\left\{\left|\frac{w_{F}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\xi,0)\right|\right.

(6.7)

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}c}{2c}\right)\left(X_{F}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)\right|v_{B}\left(X_{F}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)d\sigma\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(2r^{-2}u^{2}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\left(X_{F}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma\right\},

\displaystyle v_{B}(\xi,0)\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\frac{2u}{r}\left(X_{B}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma\right\}\left\{\left|\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\xi,0)\right|\right.

(6.8)

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c-cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}c}{2c}\right)\left(X_{B}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)\right|v_{F}\left(X_{B}(\xi,0;\sigma);t_{*}\right)d\sigma\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(2r^{-2}u^{2}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\left(X_{B}(\xi,0;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma\right\}.

Each supremum above is taken over all
s\geq 0
such that
X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)
(or
X_{F}(\xi,0;\sigma)
,
X_{B}(\xi,0;\sigma)
respectively) remains in
\Omega_{B}(t_{*})
for all
\sigma\in[0,s]
.

The goal of this section is to obtain a decay estimate of
\psi(1,\cdot)
. To this end, we shall need an estimate of the source term
W
in (
5.6
). We begin with the estimate of the nonlinearities in (
6.6
-
6.8
). These estimates rely on a coordinate transform between the forward and backward characteristics, which will be specified in the following analysis. By collecting these estimate, we will be able to measure the difference between
w_{B}(1,t)
and
w_{B}(\xi_{0}(t),0)
, which further implies the estimate of
W
. Hereby we state the expected estimates of
w_{B}
and
\psi
:

Proposition 6.16
.

Introduce the notations:

\mathcal{V}(\xi):=\left|w_{B}(\xi,0)\right|+\xi^{-1}\int_{1}^{\xi}|w_{F}(\eta,0)|d\eta+\epsilon\xi^{-3}|\psi(\xi,0)|+\epsilon^{2}\xi^{-1}\int_{1}^{\xi}\eta^{-3}|\psi(\eta,0)|d\eta,

\displaystyle\Psi(t_{*}):=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{*}}\left(1+\frac{\underline{c}}{2}(t_{*}-t)\right)^{-1}|\partial_{t}\psi(1,t)|dt+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t_{*}}\left(1+\frac{\underline{c}}{2}(t_{*}-t)\right)^{-4}|\psi(1,t)|dt

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t_{*}}\left(1+\frac{\underline{c}}{2}(t_{*}-t)\right)^{-1}|\psi(1,t)|dt,

then
w_{B}
satisfies the following bound:

\displaystyle\max_{t_{*}\in[0,T]}\left\{\xi_{0}(t_{*})\left(\left|\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(1,t_{*}\right)-\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\xi_{0}(t_{*}),0\right)\right|\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon\max_{t_{*}\in[0,T]}\xi_{0}(t_{*})\mathcal{V}(\xi_{0}(t_{*}))+\epsilon\max_{t_{*}\in[0,T]}\xi_{0}(t_{*})\Psi(t_{*})+\kappa\max_{t_{*}\in[0,T]}\left\{\xi_{0}(t_{*})\left|\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\xi_{0}(t_{*}),0)\right|\right\}.

Proposition 6.17
.

The following estimate of
Y(t)
holds for
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\left|Y(t)-Y_{0}(t)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\underline{c}t}\left\{\kappa\max_{s\in[0,T]}\left\{\xi_{0}(s)\left|\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\xi_{0}(s),0)\right|\right\}+\epsilon\max_{s\in[0,T]}\xi_{0}(s)\mathcal{V}(\xi_{0}(s))+\epsilon|Y(0)|\right\},

where

Y_{0}(t):=\exp\left[\begin{matrix}\Lambda_{1}t&\\
&\Lambda_{2}t\end{matrix}\right]Y(0)-\frac{\tilde{f}^{\prime}(1)}{c_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left[\begin{matrix}\Lambda_{1}(t-s)&\\
&\Lambda_{2}(t-s)\end{matrix}\right]\left[\begin{matrix}w_{B}(\xi_{0}(s),0)\\
w_{B}(\xi_{0}(s),0)\end{matrix}\right]ds.

Since
\psi^{2}|_{x=0}\simeq\varphi^{2}|_{x=0}
, it follows

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{2}|_{x=0}dt\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|Y_{0}(t)|^{2}dt+\left(\kappa\max_{\eta\in[1,\xi_{0}(T)]}\left\{\eta\left|\frac{w_{B}}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\eta,0)\right|\right\}+\epsilon\max_{\eta\in[1,\xi_{0}(T)]}\eta\mathcal{V}(\eta)+\epsilon|Y(0)|\right)^{2}.

6.1
Estimate of (
6.6
)

Let
X_{F}^{(\xi)}
,
X_{B}^{(\xi)}
denote the space argument of
X_{F}
,
X_{B}
respectively. By continuity, for each
\eta\in[\xi_{0}(t),\xi_{0}(T)]
, there exists a unique
\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)\in[0,T-t]
such that (figure
2
)

X_{F}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))=X_{B}(\eta,0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)),\quad\sigma_{1}(\xi_{0}(t),t)=0.

In particular, for each
t_{*}\in[t,T]
,
s_{1}(t,t_{*}):=\sigma_{1}(\xi_{0}(t_{*}),t)
satisfies

X_{F}(1,t;s_{1}(t,t_{*}))=X_{B}(\xi_{0}(t_{*}),0;t+s_{1}(t,t_{*})),

and thus the integrals in (
6.6
) is actually taken over
\sigma\in(0,s_{1}(t,t_{*}))
.
Using the bootstrap bounds (
3.1
-
3.11
), we have the following lemma for
\sigma_{1}
:

Lemma 6.18
.

For each
t\in[0,T]
,
\sigma_{1}(\cdot,t)
is a strictly increasing
C^{1}
function on
(\xi_{0}(t),\xi_{0}(T))
. Moreover, for
\eta\in(\xi_{0}(t),\xi_{0}(T))
, it holds

\left(2\overline{c}\right)^{-1}e^{-C\kappa}\leq\frac{d\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)}{d\eta}\leq\left(2\underline{c}\right)^{-1}e^{C\kappa},

1+\frac{\underline{c}e^{-C\kappa}}{\underline{c}+\overline{c}}\left(\eta-\xi_{0}(t_{*})\right)\leq X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))\leq 1+\frac{\overline{c}e^{C\kappa}}{\underline{c}+\overline{c}}(\eta-\xi_{0}(t_{*})).

Proof.

The
C^{1}
regularity of
\sigma_{1}(\cdot,t)
is given by the implicit function theorem and the
H^{2}
regularity of
c
. First, we claim that
\sigma(\eta,t)
is strictly increasing in
\eta
. If assume that
\eta_{2}>\eta_{1}
and
\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)\leq\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)
, we find

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))=X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))\leq X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))\leq X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)),

while
X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;0)>X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;0)
, which leads to contradiction with the uniqueness of integral curves. Hence
\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)>\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)
and for the same reason
X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;\tau)>X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)
for
\tau
in the common interval of
X_{B}(\eta_{1},0;\cdot)
and
X_{B}(\eta_{2},0;\cdot)
if
\eta_{2}>\eta_{1}
.
By mean value formula and (
6.3
) we have for
\eta_{2},\eta_{1}\in[\xi_{0}(t),\xi_{0}(T)]
with
\eta_{2}>\eta_{1}
that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;\tau)-X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)\right)\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\left(cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\right)(X_{B}(\eta_{1},0;\tau))-\left(cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\right)(X_{B}(\eta_{2},0;\tau))\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)^{-1}\left(X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;\tau)-X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)\right),

and thus by Gronwall’s inequality

\displaystyle(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\exp\left\{-\int_{0}^{t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)}C\epsilon X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)^{-1}d\tau\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)}C\epsilon X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)^{-1}d\tau\right\}.

Using the rough bound (
3.10
)
cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\geq\underline{c}
, we further obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)}X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)^{-1}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)}\left(X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))+\underline{c}(t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)-\tau)\right)^{-1}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\underline{c}}\log\left(1+\underline{c}(t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))\right).

Hence by the assumption (
3.11
) and that
\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)+t\leq T
, it follows that

\int_{0}^{t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)}C\epsilon X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;\tau)^{-1}d\tau\leq C\kappa,

therefore

e^{-C\kappa}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1})\leq X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))\leq e^{C\kappa}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}).

(6.9)

By the rough bound (
3.10
) again, it holds (figure
2
)

\displaystyle X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))+\underline{c}\left(\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)-\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)\right)\leq X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))\leq X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-\underline{c}\left(\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)-\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)\right),

\displaystyle X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))+\overline{c}\left(\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)-\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)\right)\geq X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))\geq X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-\overline{c}\left(\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)-\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)\right).

Combining the above inequalities yields

\displaystyle 2\underline{c}\left(\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)-\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)\right)\leq

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{C\kappa}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}),

\displaystyle 2\overline{c}\left(\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t)-\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)\right)\geq

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))-X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta_{1},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t)))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle e^{-C\kappa}(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}),

which gives the estimate of
\frac{d\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)}{d\eta}
since
\eta_{2}
,
\eta_{1}
can be arbitrary.
Note that

\displaystyle\max\left\{1+\underline{c}\sigma_{1}(\eta,t),X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta,0;t)-\overline{c}\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))=X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta,0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\min\left\{1+\overline{c}\sigma_{1}(\eta,t),X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta,0;t)-\underline{c}\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)\right\},

then taking
\eta_{2}=\eta
,
\eta_{1}=\xi_{0}(t)
in (
6.9
) yields

e^{-C\kappa}(\eta-\xi_{0}(t))\leq X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta,0;t)-1\leq e^{C\kappa}(\eta-\xi_{0}(t)).

Figure 2:
Positions of the points
X_{F}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))
,
X_{F}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta_{2},t))
and
X_{B}(\eta_{2},0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta_{1},t))

It follows

X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))\geq\left(\overline{c}+\underline{c}\right)^{-1}\left(\overline{c}+\underline{c}X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta,0;t)\right)\geq 1+\frac{\underline{c}e^{-C\kappa}}{\underline{c}+\overline{c}}(\eta-\xi_{0}(t_{*})),

and

X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))\leq\left(\overline{c}+\underline{c}\right)^{-1}\left(\underline{c}+\overline{c}X_{B}^{(\xi)}(\eta,0;t)\right)\leq 1+\frac{\overline{c}e^{C\kappa}}{\underline{c}+\overline{c}}(\eta-\xi_{0}(t_{*})).

∎

Using the bound (
3.7
) and Lemma
6.18
, we estimate the second line in (
6.6
):

\displaystyle\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(\frac{\partial_{t}c+cr^{2}\xi^{-2}\partial_{\xi}c}{2c}\right)\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)\right|v_{B}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma

(6.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-1}v_{B}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Ce^{C\kappa}\epsilon}{\underline{c}}\int_{\xi_{0}(t)}^{\xi_{0}(t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma_{1}(\eta,t))^{-1}v_{B}\left(X_{B}(\eta,0;t+\sigma_{1}(\eta,t)\right)d\eta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Ce^{C\kappa}\epsilon}{\underline{c}}\int_{\xi_{0}(t)}^{\xi_{0}(t_{*})}\left(1+\underline{c}\left(\overline{c}+\underline{c}\right)^{-1}e^{-C\kappa}\left(\eta-\xi_{0}(t)\right)\right)^{-1}v_{B}(\eta,0)d\eta.

Recall that
w_{B}=\left(\partial_{t}+cr^{2}\partial_{x}\right)\psi
. We then estimate the third line in (
6.6
) using (
3.6
):

\displaystyle\sup_{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\left(2r^{-2}u^{2}\frac{\psi}{c^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon^{2}}{\underline{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}\left|\psi\left(X_{F}(1,t;\sigma)\right)\right|d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon^{2}}{\underline{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}\left|\psi(1,t)+\int_{0}^{\sigma}w_{B}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\tau)\right)d\tau\right|d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon^{2}}{\underline{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}d\sigma\left|\psi(1,t)\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{C\epsilon^{2}\overline{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\underline{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}\int_{0}^{\sigma}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}v_{B}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\tau)\right)d\tau d\sigma,

and exchange the order of integration:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}\int_{0}^{\sigma}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}v_{B}\left(X_{F}(1,t;\tau)\right)d\tau d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}\left(\int_{\tau}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}d\sigma\right)v_{B}\left(x_{F}(1,t;\tau)\right)d\tau.

Use the rough bound (
3.10
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\sigma)^{-4}d\sigma\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{s_{1}(t,t_{*})}\left(X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\tau)+\underline{c}(\sigma-\tau)\right)^{-4}d\sigma\leq\frac{1}{3\underline{c}}X_{F}^{(\xi)}(1,t;\tau)^{-3}.
